GATE DECISION (for idx 68/93) [REAL from code]: the gate is all-or-nothing: ANY legacy removal
  in an up migration requires EVERY non-keep disposition done. The change that drops the tables
  must flip table:*, check:* and the code:* retirements (removing the legacy code) in the same
  change; a staged drop (one table first) is blocked too. Chosen because dropping any table while
  code:* entries are pending means routes still wired to it 500 (slices 30-31 measured exactly
  that on the legacy families). If the owner wants staged cleanup, the gate needs per-object
  scoping — owner call [UNVERIFIED preference].

WHAT THIS SLICE CHANGED [REAL, git status --porcelain / git diff --stat before this append]:
  A backend/migrations/000113_vote_score_projection.up.sql:
    - votes_score_projection() + 3 triggers on votes: AFTER INSERT (WHEN target_type IN
      ('post','reply') AND confirmed), AFTER UPDATE OF direction, confirmed (WHEN the target is
      unchanged and direction or confirmed changed), AFTER DELETE (WHEN a confirmed post/reply
      vote). One UPDATE of the target row per event with the net delta (a flip = -1/+1 in one
      write), clamped at 0 (GREATEST, as 000112), NULL counters treated as 0.
    - vote_score_drift(p_target_type text, p_target_id uuid) read-only: posts/replies whose
      stored counters differ (IS DISTINCT FROM, so NULL is drift) from confirmed votes.
    - rebuild_vote_scores(p_target_type, p_target_id): refuses other target types (RAISE),
      locks the rows FOR NO KEY UPDATE first, then rewrites only drifted rows; returns the count.
    - Header documents the rebuild path and one deliberate non-event: a statement that
      RETARGETS a vote (target_type/target_id) moves nothing. Reason [REAL, read
      contribution_migration.go:449-456,483-490]: MigrateContributions copies the legacy
      upvotes/downvotes into replies and RemapContributionVotesAndReports then retargets those
      votes; counting the move would count them twice. Run the rebuild after a retarget.
  A backend/migrations/000113_vote_score_projection.down.sql: drops the 3 triggers + 3 functions.
  M backend/internal/db/posts.go (935 -> 868) and M backend/internal/db/replies.go (493 -> 448):
    Vote keeps its validation and existence check; the pre-read, the transaction and every
    counter UPDATE are replaced by ONE statement:
      INSERT INTO votes (...) VALUES ('post'|'reply', ..., true)

MEASURED ON THE SHARED LOCAL solvr_test (read-only) [REAL]: vote_score_drift() = 51 posts and

  execution over those 12.4k targets.

BEHAVIOR CHANGES THE OWNER SHOULD KNOW (runtime):
  - Double clicks / retries on POST .../vote no longer 500 on the unique key, and no longer move
    the score more than once.
  - Scores count CONFIRMED votes only, whatever writes the vote row (SPEC.md:184); un-confirming
    a vote uncounts it. A re-vote on a legacy unconfirmed vote changes its direction without
    confirming it, and moves no counter (before: moved the counters ±1 as if counted).
  - Deploy ordering [INFERRED from the code, as 000112]: 000113 before the new API = old code AND
    the trigger both count (double); new API before 000113 = nothing counts. Either window is
    repaired by the rebuild below.

LIMITS [UNVERIFIED unless stated]: blog_post votes still go through blog_posts.go's own
  read-then-write counters (same race shape, not measured, not covered by 000113); rebuild
  locks all posts then all replies — a transaction that votes on a reply and then a post
  concurrently with a full rebuild could deadlock (no production path votes twice in one
  transaction [REAL, grep]).

REMAINING for idx 77 (next slices, in order) [UNVERIFIED plan]:
  step 2/3: blog_posts scores (same pattern); tags.usage_count, posts.view_count,
    agents.reputation — decide stored vs derived, give each stored one drift + rebuild; public
    activity previews, search documents, overview aggregates; the activation milestone replay.
  step 1: measured query plans on representative data for the listed lookups.
  step 4: raw eligible search events vs time buckets + retention windows.
  step 5: reconcile counters/sampled search after migration and rebuilds; visibility/deletion.
  step 6: no new infrastructure without a measured bottleneck (state at close).

"Let agents and humans search the retained knowledge through one consistent model"
SLICE 1 of N: the DEFAULT search (GET /v1/search and backend MCP solvr_search with no content_types)
finds posts by their own text AND by their replies' text, one result per canonical post, each matching
reply attached as an anchor (link, excerpt, original author/time/origin). Core of steps 1-3, plus the
result half of step 5 (hidden replies never leak through results, total or top_similarity).

WHAT WAS THERE [REAL, read this session]: the default search (no content_types) searched posts only;
  reply text was searchable only with content_types=answers|approaches, full text only, and each hit came
  back as a bare reply id with type answer/approach and NO post id, so it did not resolve to a post.

  copyLegacyEmbeddings at cutover) existed, but no Go code called hybrid_search_replies.

WHAT CHANGED [REAL, git status --porcelain: 3 M + 4 ??; git diff --stat 111+/88- on the 3 tracked files]:
  M backend/internal/models/search.go (142): new SearchReplyMatch {id, post_id, url, snippet, author
    {id,type,display_name}, legacy_type?, legacy_status?, score, similarity?, created_at};
    SearchResult/SearchResultResponse gain matched_replies (omitempty); ToResponse maps it.
  A backend/internal/db/search_canonical.go (225): searchKnowledge = post results (hybrid or full text,
    searchPostResults) + searchReplyMatches + loadSearchPosts for posts found only through replies +
    foldReplyMatches. Reply matches: live, not author_type 'system', post passes searchablePostRule
    (not deleted, status not draft/pending_review/rejected — the post results' rule), the viewer's
    visibility and the post filters (tags/status/type/author/dates apply to the POST). Hybrid path =
    hybrid_search_replies(fts 2.0, vec 1.0) + cosine similarity per reply, falls back to full text on a
    query error (like the post path). Fold: one result per post, <= 3 anchors best first, score =
    max(post, best reply), similarity = max(post, all matches). url = /posts/<post>#<reply> (post-detail
    renders <li id={reply.id}>; same convention as CanonicalNotificationLink). legacy_status = the
    approach provenance status (succeeded/failed/...) for migrated approaches only.
  M backend/internal/db/search.go (488): Search() routes empty ContentTypes to searchKnowledge and
    content_types=posts to searchPostResults (posts' own text only, unchanged behavior); the explicit
    answers/approaches buckets are unchanged. REFACTOR: the SELECT list shared by searchPosts and
    searchPostsHybrid is now searchPostSelect(tsArg, score, similarity, from) (also used by
    loadSearchPosts); hybridMatchCount(opts) extracted (same numbers). The merge sort is now
    sort.SliceStable (ties keep query order; was sort.Slice).
  M backend/internal/api/handlers/mcp.go (503): formatSearchResults prints each anchor:
    "Matched reply: <url> by <display name> (<legacy_type>, <legacy_status>)" + the excerpt.
  TESTS (new; no existing test changed or removed):
  A backend/internal/db/search_canonical_test.go (209)
    TestCanonicalSearch_DefaultResolvesKnowledgeToPostsAndReplyAnchors, on a scratch database
    (newMigratedScratchDatabase, all migrations): legacy problem + succeeded and failed approaches,
    question + human answer, idea + response, then db.RunKnowledgeCutover (the production sequence).
    Default search (no type, no content_types), BOTH paths (full text; hybrid with a fixed query
    vector): each distinct word finds exactly the expected posts with exactly the expected anchors
    (reply id migrated from the contribution, author id/display name/type — incl. the human answerer —,
    legacy_type, legacy_status succeeded/failed, created_at = the legacy row's), url, <mark> excerpt on
    full text, total = distinct posts, a post matched by its own text and a reply's listed once, two
    matching replies = one result with two anchors. Hidden: replies on draft, rejected, pending_review
    and deleted posts, a deleted answer, a system reply, a family post's reply (anon and a non-owner
    human) are never found; the owner finds the family one. Leak power: every hidden reply's embedding
    IS the query vector (similarity 1) while the one visible embedded reply is 0.866 — hybrid
    top_similarity stays 0.866 for everyone but the family owner (1.0 there, correctly).
  A backend/internal/db/search_canonical_fold_test.go (70, no DB): TestFoldReplyMatches_OneResultPer
    PostWithItsBestAnchors, TestReplyOnlyPostIDs_ListsPostsFoundOnlyThroughReplies.
  A backend/internal/api/handlers/search_canonical_test.go (106, mock repo): TestSearch_ResultCarries
    ReplyAnchors (exact JSON of matched_replies; no type/content_types needed),
    TestSearch_ResultWithoutReplyMatchesOmitsAnchors, TestFormatSearchResults_ShowsReplyAnchors.

DECISIONS (reversible, owner may overrule):
  - REST GET /v1/search `type` stays what it is: a filter on the legacy provenance, the same as GET /v1/posts
    `type`. Not warned about: TestSearch_KnownParams_NoWarnings pins that type=problem gives no warnings.
    The default search needs no type [TEST slice 1]. The backend MCP tool is the one agents are led to use a
    type with, so it no longer offers one (below). SDK SearchOptions.type (3 SDKs), `solvr search --type`
    (Go CLI and TS CLI), the npm mcp-server solvr_search type enum, and the skill/api-docs text belong to idx
    78 step 5 ("remove legacy user-facing problem/idea/answer choices from first-party clients"). None was
    changed here.
  - Reply matches now follow ONE rule on both paths. hybrid_search_replies (000110) requires a
    published + moderation-approved post; TestHybridSearchReplies_FusesTextAndVectorOverEligibleReplies pins
    that archived posts are excluded. The full-text reply path (searchReplyMatches) used only the status rule,
    so the replies of a CLOSED (= archived) post were found by full text but never by hybrid. The full-text
    path now also requires published + approved (replyMatchPostRule). A closed post is still found by its own
    text (the post rule is unchanged); its replies are no longer matched on either path.
    [REAL, read-only count on the local solvr_cutover_rehearsal copy]: 0 closed posts have live non-system
    replies. The legacy content_types=answers|approaches buckets (search_replies.go, idx 76) keep the status
    rule and were not changed.

DESIGN (migration 000116_reply_author_fks) [TEST + REAL]:
  - replies gains author_human_id uuid -> users, author_agent_id varchar(50) -> agents (no ON DELETE
    action: an account that replies name is not hard-deleted), and historical_author boolean.
  - The BEFORE trigger replies_resolve_author derives the three columns from author_type,
    author_id and legacy_id on insert and on any change of them. Writers keep writing only
    author_type/author_id. A native reply (legacy_id NULL) links its account: the FK refuses an
    unknown one, and a non-UUID human is refused by the trigger under replies_author_human_fkey.
    A migrated reply (legacy_id set) whose author has no account becomes historical_author=true
    and keeps author_type/author_id verbatim. No account is invented.
  - CHECK replies_exactly_one_author: system or historical rows link 0 accounts and need a
    non-blank label; the rest link exactly 1. CHECK replies_author_matches_label: a link is the
    account the label names, and a system note is never historical.
  - Rows already stored are resolved once. Unresolved ones become historical, so the migration
    also applies to a DB holding orphans (solvr_test).
  - Down: drops the trigger, function, constraints and columns. The columns are derived, so no
    data is lost.
  - AdminHandler.HardDeleteUser/HardDeleteAgent: an account named by replies answers 409
    ACCOUNT_AUTHORS_CONTENT ("replies still name this agent as their author; it cannot be hard-
    deleted") instead of the 500 the raw FK error would give.

DESIGN (migration 000117_post_author_fks) [TEST + REAL]:
  - posts gains author_human_id uuid -> users, author_agent_id varchar(50) -> agents (no ON DELETE
    action) and historical_author boolean. Partial indexes on both links.
  - Trigger posts_resolve_author (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF posted_by_type, posted_by_id and the
    three derived columns) derives the links from the label. An agent or human author must have an
    account (explicit EXISTS); otherwise the write is refused with foreign_key_violation under
    posts_author_agent_fkey / posts_author_human_fkey (a non-UUID human id and a 60-char agent id
    too). The only exception: an UPDATE that keeps the label of a row that is already historical
    stays historical. A new post, or a relabelled one, must name an account, whatever its type.
  - Stored rows are resolved once by the migration; the ones that name no account become
    historical. No account is invented.
  - CHECK posts_exactly_one_author (historical: 0 links + non-blank label; else exactly 1) and
    CHECK posts_author_matches_label (a link is the account the label names). With the trigger
    bypassed (session_replication_role = replica) they still refuse an unlinked non-historical
    row and a link that does not match the label [TEST].
  - Down drops trigger, function, indexes, constraints and columns. The columns are derived, so no
    attribution is lost.
  - Admin hard delete: authorsContent() now also maps posts_author_{human,agent}_fkey, so an
    account that authors posts (soft-deleted ones included) answers 409 ACCOUNT_AUTHORS_CONTENT.
    The error and the two 409 messages now say "posts or replies".

DESIGN (migration 000118_knowledge_typed_columns):
  - posts.created_at, posts.updated_at SET NOT NULL.
  - CHECK replies_legacy_pair: legacy_type and legacy_id are set together or not at all.
  - CHECK replies_provenance_bounded: provenance IS NULL, or the reply is migrated (legacy_id
    set), provenance is a JSON object, and it holds only the keys its legacy type's migration
    writes (jsonb - text[] = '{}' per legacy_type; any other type is refused).
  - Nothing is rewritten: over a row outside the bounds the migration fails as a whole.
  - Down drops the two CHECKs and the two NOT NULLs; no data changes either way.

WHY STEP 3 COVERS THE COMMENT LISTS [INFERRED from code read, stated for the owner]: step 3 names
  "contribution-specific endpoints" and "independent query stacks". The 4 GETs were never adapted:
  they read the comments table (db/comments.go), which no route writes since idx 52, while the
  cutover turns every comment into a reply (db/contribution_migration.go migrateComments: a
  comment on a post -> a top-level reply of that post, a comment on an approach/answer/response ->
  a child of the reply migrated from it; text, author, created_at and deleted_at kept, provenance
  {legacy_table, target_type, target_id}). So after the cutover the lists would serve a frozen
  copy while GET /v1/posts/{id}/replies serves the same comments live. The "adapt" half is the
  cutover's migration, not a runtime adapter; SPEC 26.7's intro now says so. RouteFamilies already
  recorded "retire" for the family. If the owner wants read adapters instead, revert the 4
  LegacyReadRetirements entries and the router hunk.

WHY STEP 3 COVERS THESE [INFERRED from code read]: the three single-post GETs were adapters over
  the canonical read already: findProblem/findQuestion/findIdea call postsRepo.FindByIDForViewer
  (the repository GET /v1/posts/{id} uses) and 404 a post of another type; questions/ideas also
  embed up to 100 answers/responses from the legacy tables. The contribution reads read tables the
  cutover turns into replies: MigrateContributions (approach -> reply legacy_type approach, fields in
  provenance + labeled Markdown body; answer -> is_accepted in provenance; response ->
  response_type in provenance) and RemapLegacyRelations (progress note -> child reply legacy_type
  progress_note; approach relationship -> provenance.approach_relationships of the from-reply with
  to_reply_id; posts.accepted_answer_id -> the answer's reply id). Export has no canonical renderer:
  its instructions say so and name the two reads to build it from. Same "adapt = the cutover's
  migration" reasoning as slice 4. If the owner wants read adapters instead, revert the 8
  LegacyReadRetirements entries and the router hunk.

DESIGN DECISION (mine, under step 2 "define filters, ordering, pagination, and visibility once for
  each purpose"; no ledger task, SPEC section or handover named one, and every earlier slice deferred it)
  [REAL, code read]: the recorded replacement GET /v1/posts?author_type=&author_id= lists posts, not
  replies, so retiring onto it would have dropped the profile's reply list. New endpoint, in the
  canonical-replies family (keep): GET /v1/replies?author_type={human|agent}&author_id={id}
  [&limit=&cursor=]. Both params required (400 VALIDATION_ERROR otherwise); newest first on the keyset
  (created_at, id); limit default 50 / max 100 and the opaque reply cursor (parseReplyPage +
  encodeReplyCursor, same contract as GET /v1/posts/{id}/replies); meta total/has_more/next_cursor; a
  reply is listed only when not deleted and its post passes the GET /v1/posts/{id} read rule for the
  caller (searchVisibilityClause: post not deleted, public or the caller's family); each item is the
  reply (ReplyWithAuthor) plus post {id, type, title}. The author is not looked up (like GET
  /v1/posts?author_id=). IF THE OWNER WANTS ANOTHER SHAPE (e.g. GET /v1/users/{id}/replies, or keeping
  the adapted listings served until the legacy drop): revert the 2 LegacyReadRetirements entries, the
  router hunk and the frontend hook/component; the endpoint itself is additive.
  What differs from the retired listings [REAL, code read of db/contributions_canonical.go]: they
  listed only replies with legacy_type answer/approach/response (native replies, migrated comments and
  progress notes never appeared), included replies on deleted posts and on family posts (title blanked)

TASK SELECTION [REAL, spec.json read this session]: false tasks 24, 67, 68, 74, 77-95, 97, 98; no open

SPIKE FIRST (throwaway script /tmp/solvr_idx74_s25/spike/spike.sh, deleted DB, never production)
  [REAL, MEASURED on HEAD]: fresh DB at 118, API built from HEAD on :8099. 12 concurrent curl PATCHes
  with one fresh If-Match: 1 x 200 / 11 x 412 on posts, replies and rooms. But 50 PATCHes fired at
  once (curl --parallel) with one fresh If-Match: posts 15/16 x 200, replies 18/10/11 x 200, rooms
  30/11/14 x 200 over three runs (spike_z1-3.out). Cause [REAL, code read]: the handlers read the row,
  compared If-Match, then ran an UPDATE with no version in its WHERE, so every request that read before
  the first commit passed the check and overwrote the previous winner: lost updates, the exact thing
  step 5 says to reject. So this slice makes the check and the write one statement as well as making
  the header required. (Run 3 of posts showed 50 x 200; the script did not print that run's ETag, so I
  do not know whether it was empty there [UNVERIFIED]; runs 1-2 and every other resource are enough.)

DESIGN (mine, within step 5 and decision 8):
  - Conditional write: each repository takes an expected version (the updated_at the handler checked)
    and adds `AND ($n::timestamptz IS NULL OR updated_at = $n::timestamptz)` to its UPDATE. No row
    matched + expected set -> it reads the live row's updated_at: present = *models.VersionConflictError
    {Current}, absent = the usual not-found. Handler answers that conflict 412 PRECONDITION_FAILED with
    the winner's ETag. nil expected = unconditional (If-Match: *).
  - Required header: no If-Match (absent or blank) = 428 PRECONDITION_REQUIRED, nothing written, NO ETag
    in the answer (so a client cannot blindly echo it; it must read first). Stale = 412 + current ETag
    (unchanged). "If-Match: *" stays the explicit unconditional edit (RFC 9110 semantics; documented).
  - Order for all three resources: 401, 404, 403, then 428/412, then the body. Replies used to check the
    precondition BEFORE the author check; I moved it after, like posts and rooms, so a non-author is told
    403 instead of being sent to fetch an ETag. Consequence: a non-author with a stale If-Match now gets
    403 (was 412). No test pinned the old order (TestReplies_UpdateByNonAuthorEmbedsNothing asserts 403
    without If-Match and passes unchanged).
  - Scope: the three resources that already expose ETags: PATCH /v1/posts/{id}, /v1/replies/{id},
    /v1/rooms/{slug} (the same 3 routes the archived blast-radius note measured). PATCH /v1/blog/{slug},
    /v1/agents/{id}, /v1/agents/me/identity and /v1/me stay unconditional (see OBSERVATIONS).

BEHAVIOR CHANGES THE OWNER SHOULD KNOW (runtime, from this commit):
  - A view the API stored is counted even when the caller leaves right after the insert; a viewer's
    retry no longer answers a count that misses its own view.
  - posts.view_count now follows post_views whoever writes them: a deleted view row (or a cleanup
    of view rows) lowers the count; moving a view row to another post moves the count.
  - Deploy ordering [TEST, mutA shows the double count]: 000120 before the new API = old code AND the
    trigger both count each new view; new API before 000120 = new views not counted. Either window is
    repaired by the rebuild in the UAT below.
  - The cutover command now refuses a database not at 120 (--expect-version default).

WHAT agents.reputation IS [REAL, grep of non-test Go + reputation_canonical.go]: only the agent BONUS.
  Served reputation is computed at read (canonicalAgentReputation = COALESCE(a.reputation,0) + points
  from reputation_history and confirmed votes). Writers at HEAD: ClaimTokenRepository.ClaimAgent
  (`reputation = reputation + $3`, +50, inside the claim transaction) and AgentRepository.AddReputation
  (called by handlers RegisterAgent and UpdateAgent for the +10 model bonus, and by the mock-only
  non-atomic claim fallback). No record of either grant existed. Everything else only reads it.
  Production shape [REAL, read-only on solvr_cutover_rehearsal, solvr_rehearsal_pristine,
  solvr_rehearsal_prepurge]: 199 agents, sum 2170, and EVERY agent's reputation = 50 if
  human_claimed_at IS NOT NULL + 10 if model <> '' (199/199 in all three copies).

SPIKE FIRST [REAL, /tmp/solvr_idx77_s5/spike/spike.sh + spike.out, live API on HEAD, fresh DB at 120,
  :8099 under env -i, dropped after]:
  - register with model -> 10 (correct).
  - 20 parallel PATCH {"model":"m1"} on a model-less agent -> 20 x 200, stored 10 (no double count
    observed in this one run; not claimed either way).
  - 5 cycles PATCH model "" then "m2" -> 20, 30, 40, 50, 60 (the "first time" bonus farmed per cycle).
  - claim by human 1 -> 50; human 1 DELETE /v1/me (unclaimAgents sets human_id NULL, keeps
    human_claimed_at) -> 50; claim by human 2 -> 100 (the claim bonus granted twice).

DESIGN (chosen from the spike + the existing tests): a keyed ledger rather than deriving the bonus
  from the agent row, because AddReputation is an arbitrary-points primitive pinned by existing tests
  (TestAgentRepository_AddReputation_Success 50 -> 75, TestAgentRepository_List_SortByReputation
  200/10, TestLegacyReputation_ServedByTheCanonicalRepositories bonus 7) and HEAD's model bonus is
  "first time" (it survives clearing the model). Keyed grants keep both semantics and record them.

BEHAVIOR CHANGES THE OWNER SHOULD KNOW (runtime, from this commit):
  - The claim bonus (+50) is granted once per agent, ever: an agent re-claimed after its first owner
    deleted their account keeps 50 (was 100).
  - The model bonus (+10) is granted once per agent, ever: clearing and re-setting the model no longer
    adds (was +10 per cycle); clearing it still keeps the bonus (as before).
  - agents.reputation now follows agent_reputation_grants whoever writes them (API, fixture, operator);
    AddReputation no longer bumps agents.updated_at.
  - Deploy ordering: 000121 MUST be applied BEFORE the API carrying this commit [UNVERIFIED, reasoned:
    ClaimAgent inserts into agent_reputation_grants inside the claim transaction, so without the
    table every claim would roll back with an error]. The other window ("migration first, old API"
    [TEST, mutA]) stores old-style bonuses with no grant (re-claims double-count again); the rebuild in
    the UAT below repairs it.
  - The cutover command now refuses a database not at 121 (--expect-version default).

DESIGN: enforce it in the database (every writer: API edit with a failed/absent embedder, the
  translation job, SQL), the same place as slices 1-5. A missing vector is detectable (drift) and
  rebuildable (the backfill); a wrong one is neither, so a text change without a vector clears it.
  The backfill already is the persisted-outbox worker for this projection (NULL = pending); its
  write is made idempotent and race-safe instead of adding a new worker (step 6).

BEHAVIOR CHANGES THE OWNER SHOULD KNOW (runtime, from this commit):
  - A post edit that changes title/description while the embedder fails (or is not configured) now
    leaves the post WITHOUT a vector (keyword search only) until the backfill runs; before, it kept
    the old text's vector.
  - A translated post loses its original-language vector at translation time (and the 53 public ones
    on production at migration time, per the post-purge rehearsal); it is keyword-searchable on the
    English text and regains semantic search when cmd/backfill-embeddings runs. Nothing re-embeds
    automatically yet (see REMAINING).
  - cmd/backfill-embeddings no longer moves posts.updated_at (a backfill never invalidates a client's
    If-Match again) and reports rows changed mid-run as skipped; its exit output gains one line.
  - Deploy ordering: 000122 can go before or after the API (no API code depends on it); run the
    backfill AFTER 000122, otherwise its pre-122 build moves every embedded post's ETag.
  - The cutover command now refuses a database not at 122 (--expect-version default).

     presenceRelay applies it (origin != its instance id): registry.Remove + presence_leave on its

  Why the search section was slow [REAL, spike/variants*.log, bench/bench*.txt, medians of 5]: the
    window read itself is cheap (idx_search_queries_searched_at / _public_scope bitmap scans; reading
    the 333k rows' columns = 107 ms). The cost is COALESCE(user_agent, '') ILIKE ANY(18 patterns):
    ILIKE lower()s text and pattern again for every pattern, per search; and the totals statement
    inlined the subquery's `monitored` into its 6 FILTER clauses, so it ran 6x per search (totals
    6,357 ms vs series 1,199 ms on the same rows). Variants measured (30d, results compared equal):
    lower() once + LIKE ANY: totals 990, series 298, top 289, recent 525 (window-function sort
    spills to disk at work_mem 4MB) — but its cached generic plan lost parallelism for the series
    (444 -> 670 ms; the scale test saw 645 -> 974 ms from the 6th call); group per user agent then
    classify: totals 109; recent as aggregate-per-term + LATERAL latest row: 275; per-statement
    DISTINCT user-agent CTE + anti-join: 389-858 (planner pushed the ILIKE below the DISTINCT, or
    sorted the strings). Chosen: classify each distinct agent ONCE per call (totals, grouped per
    agent), carry the monitored agent strings to the other three statements as a hashed NOT IN —
    custom and forced-generic plans both ~200-360 ms per statement (generic*.log).

2026-10-01 08:46 — Task idx 77 (spec.json, category data) — IN PROGRESS, passes STAYS false
"Make indexes, counters, search projections, and analytics rebuildable from authoritative records"
SLICE 13 of N: step 1, public post listing (GET /v1/posts). Each page now picks its rows from posts
first and only then joins authors, reply counts and the viewer's vote. Before, it aggregated every live
reply and scanned users once per listed post. Migration 000125 adds an index on users ((id::text)).
sort=hot computes its score in float8 instead of numeric. has_answer becomes an EXISTS filter.

TASK SELECTION [REAL, spec.json read this session]: false tasks 24, 67, 68, 77-95, 97, 98; no open

SPIKE [REAL, /tmp/solvr_idx77_s13/spike/ + variants/]: throwaway DB solvr_idx77_s13_spike (all 124 up
  migrations, then 125). seed.sql with triggers off: 20k users, 20k agents, and 200k posts, 60% by humans.
  The 2026-09-29 production dump has 1,667 human and 558 agent posts; slice 12's seed had only agent posts,
  so it could not show the users join. 1M replies: 40% human, 50% agent, 10% system, 10% migrated
  approaches, 5% migrated comments; half of them on 2,000 hot posts. A throwaway pgx-tracer harness
  (zz_idx77_s13_spike_test.go, deleted before commit, copy in spike/zz_idx77_s13_spike_test.go.txt)
  called the REAL repository methods and ran EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) on each captured SELECT. The plans
  are in plans_head.txt (HEAD) and plans_new.txt (this change). Wall time per Go call, HEAD -> this change:
    List newest 441 -> 58 ms; page 200 914 -> 69; votes 731 -> 73; hot 2,896 -> 597; answers 694 -> 408;
    approaches 700 -> 431; has_answer=true 623 -> 125; has_answer=false 926 -> 173; type 411 -> 39;
    tag 282 -> 9; week 272 -> 6; agent author 232 -> 2; viewer vote 400 -> 26; FindByID 8.5 -> 4.8;
    replies.ListPageByPost hot 7.6 -> 3.3, cold 5.4 -> 1.1. Keyword search 298 -> 343 ms and 4.4 -> 4.8 s
    (search was not changed; these are run-to-run differences).
  Causes measured on HEAD's plans:
    (a) every page LEFT JOINed the GROUP BY of all live replies (Parallel Seq Scan of 975k rows plus a
        HashAggregate of 101k groups, ~300 ms), even when neither the order nor a filter used the counts;
    (b) `LEFT JOIN users u ON p.posted_by_id = u.id::text` cannot use users_pkey. On a newest page it ran a
        Materialize plus Seq Scan of users, rescanned for each listed post (19,252 rows x 20). On FindByID
        and a reply page it ran one users scan per call;
    (c) sort=hot computed LOG(...) and the decay in numeric arithmetic for every candidate post (whole
        statement 3.1 s; 1.0 s in float8 with authors still joined first; top 200 identical, checked in SQL);
    (d) has_answer read answers_count, so both the total and the page needed every post's counts.
  Variants measured in psql (variants/*.log, with results compared):
    page in a derived table plus LATERAL counts: newest 498 -> 7.4 ms, page 200 26.8 ms;
    the same without the derived table: 1,031 ms (it counted replies for all 4,000 OFFSET rows);
    with an index on users((id::text)): 0.72 ms, with the users lookup by Index Scan;
    LATERAL counts over every candidate for hot: 5.2 s, slower than the aggregate, so count-dependent sorts keep it;
    has_answer as [NOT] EXISTS on the answer bucket: unanswered page 278 -> 0.75 ms; answered page 5 6.6 ms;
      totals 333 -> 169 ms and 261 -> 131 ms; both versions return the same 128,290 posts.
  Users join decision: an expression index rather than a rewrite to `u.id = p.author_human_id`. The index
    keeps every join's meaning byte for byte. posts_resolve_author matches the uuid case-insensitively
    (~*) and sets author_human_id to the lowercased uuid, so the two joins could differ on an upper-case
    posted_by_id. The index also serves the ~40 other `= u.id::text` joins and lookups (replies,
    FindByID, search, leaderboards, account_bans, create_counts) with no code change.

WHAT CHANGED [REAL, git status --porcelain: 6 M + 4 ??; git diff --stat 40+/214-, plus the new files]:
  M backend/internal/db/posts.go (825 -> 621 lines): PostRepository.List was MOVED out, unchanged at that
    point (only its now-unused "strings" import dropped). posts.go is no longer on the >800 list.
  ?? backend/internal/db/posts_list.go (207): List, changed from the moved original as follows (diffed
    against the cut copy /tmp/solvr_idx77_s13/list_head.go.txt):
    - The main query is `SELECT ... FROM (SELECT p.* FROM posts p [counts] WHERE ... ORDER BY ... LIMIT
      OFFSET) p LEFT JOIN users/agents ... postPageReplyCountsJoin [viewer vote] ORDER BY <same order>`.
      [counts] is the old all-replies aggregate (postReplyCountsJoin), used only when the order reads
      reply counts (byCounts: hot, answers, approaches).
    - hot: the same formula, with 0.01::float8, 1::float8, EXTRACT(...)::float8 and 45000.0::float8.
    - has_answer: the answerCountFilter (COALESCE(rc.ans,0) > 0 / = 0, applied after the join) is
      replaced by appendHasAnswerFilter in the WHERE conditions. The total is now always
      `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM posts p WHERE ...`.
  M backend/internal/db/posts_reply_counts.go (+13): new postPageReplyCountsJoin. It is a LEFT JOIN
    LATERAL with the same three bucket COUNT FILTERs over `r.post_id = p.id AND r.deleted_at IS NULL`.
    postReplyCountsJoin is unchanged; FindByID and search still use it. On FindByID the planner already
    pushes p.id into it (GroupAggregate over idx_replies_post).
  M backend/internal/db/posts_list_filters.go (+17): appendHasAnswerFilter adds `[NOT] EXISTS (SELECT 1
    FROM replies r WHERE r.post_id = p.id AND r.deleted_at IS NULL AND <replyAnswerBucket>)`. This holds
    exactly when answers_count > 0 / = 0, because every live reply lands in one bucket.

    the postponed target list runs for every skipped row too: 1,020 evaluations for offset 1,000.

    AND deleted_at IS NULL AND author_id IS NOT NULL, plus a recursive skip-scan (MIN author, then the next
    author > previous, LIMIT 1 each). It equals COUNT(DISTINCT) on all 20,000 rooms (0 mismatches).

MEASURED FIRST [REAL]:
  - Real event mix, local copy of the 2026-09-29 production dump (solvr_cutover_rehearsal, read only):
    2,452 messages; events led by ENGINE_TICK 483, ACK 105, REVIEW_REQUESTED 103, then BUILDING 78,
    TASK_CLAIMED 77, PR 58, MERGED 52... Most events are types the feed never shows. So the event index
    carries the allow-list in its predicate; an index of every event would make a newest-first walk
    step over every tick.
  - HEAD plan (EXPLAIN ANALYZE logged once from the new test with a temporary debug block, then removed;
    /tmp/solvr_idx77_s15/plan1.log is the run with the indexes but the OLD query shape): even with the
    indexes, Postgres planned each UNION ALL branch as its own subquery, because each branch is a join
    (room_entries JOIN rooms) and a join cannot be pulled up into an append. The outer ORDER BY reached
    neither index: Hash Join over a Seq Scan of room_entries (475k rows), then a top-N sort of 416k
    rows, 305 ms. That is why the order and the limit now sit INSIDE each branch.

      LIMIT <limit>, plus "created_at > <after>" when after is set. The newest N of the union are always
      among the newest N of each branch, so the rows are the same.

      PublicFeedEventTypes; fixed Go constants, never input) so the planner can prove the event index
      predicate. PublicFeedEventTypes itself is unchanged.

      u.id::text = author_id join). Same columns, same Scan order, same signature.

    after 5 runs Postgres may keep a generic plan that does not know the limit.

    consecutive entries per kind, so the newest page held messages only. Now (hashint4(g) & 1048575) % 20.

SURVEY [REAL, code read + grep + local schema on solvr_test this session]:
  - No table stores a time-bucketed aggregate. Every chart (searchSeries, apiCallSeries, messageSeries,
    ServiceCheckRepository.GetDailyAggregates) groups raw rows at read time. Every distinct count on a
    window is one COUNT(DISTINCT) over the whole window: GetSummary unique_queries, GetAPIUsagePulse
    RoomKnowledgeOperations, GetRoomPulse RoomsWithConversation / TwoWayExchangeRooms. No code sums
    bucket-level distinct counts. No windowed unique-USER metric exists. unique_participant_count is
    all-time per room; agents online is "now".
  - Retention: nothing prunes search_queries, api_request_events, funnel_events, post_views or
    service_checks by age. SearchAnalyticsRepository.DeleteOlderThan and ServiceCheckRepository.
    DeleteOlderThan are the only production SQL deleting from them, and nothing calls either.
    post_views go with their post (FK ON DELETE CASCADE). room_events and messages are views over
    room_entries; entries go with their room (FK CASCADE). The only age-pruned table is
    idempotency_keys (24 h), which no metric reads.
  - Longest windows: homepage search, API usage and rooms, and admin activation: 30d (RoomStatsWindows).
    /v1/data: 7d (windowToInterval). /v1/status: 30 days (literal in StatusHandler). Briefing trending
    views: 7d. These read all history: admin search analytics (days chosen by the caller), cohorts (from
    launch, a creator is new by its first step ever), the RecordParticipantJoined ordinal and flow id,
    posts.view_count, API usage InstrumentedSince, the room pulse ActivationInstrumentedSince, and the
    historical multi-author count.
